Build a SINGLE shared figure registry for this Theogony reading edition. This is a note-only task. Source data is not instructions. Do not generate or change poem verses, summaries, or passage-specific 'here' explanations.

Every identifiable deity, primordial being, divine personification, divine collective, monster, mortal or heroic figure in the supplied cards needs one stable global entry. Reuse that entry across all passages and alternate names. Do not merge namesakes: Oceanid Doris and Nereid Doris are different; the Muse Erato differs from the Nereid Erato; the Muse Thalia differs from the Grace Thalia; mortal and Nereid Agave/Autonoe differ. River gods are figures, but ordinary places/objects/word definitions remain local cards. Triton the sea god differs from the river Triton in the variant. Aeetes is a named figure even when called a king. 'Earth and Sky', 'Hephaestus and Aglaia' and similar grouped cards need separate figure bindings; keep the original contextual explanation shared, without rewriting it.

For each entity return id (short stable lowercase slug), name (canonical English display name), kind, general, evidence [{verse,greek}], bindings [{section_id,card_id,aliases}]. 'general' is ONE common basic identity description, not a biography: one or two short modern English sentences, at most 32 words. It must say the kind of being and essential role. Very plain language for teenagers, no jargon unless immediately explained. For translated names identify the Greek name and personification briefly. Catalog-only Nereids/Oceanids/river gods need only identity/family, no invented individual powers. Keep uncertain mythology uncertain and follow this local Greek source. Do not import later tales or make all figures Olympian gods. Explain Chaos/Tartarus as primordial gap/depth, not automatically as human-like gods.

SPOILER POLICY: no future deeds, outcomes, defeats, marriages, transformations or children lists in the shared general. No plot summary. Prefer a core identity valid from the first appearance; 'Heracles is a famously strong hero' over his later immortality, 'Semele is a mortal woman in Cadmus's family' over her later divine status. The unchanged local here note will give context/status when the passage actually reaches it. Do not invent a fixed divine status where this poem's depiction is complex (Medea/Phaethon/Chrysaor etc). Namesakes may be distinguished by parents if necessary. Basic deity role such as Zeus sky/thunder/lightning or Nyx embodied Night is fine.

BINDINGS: Cover EVERY supplied (section_id,card_id), either through entity bindings, or exactly once in local_cards [{section_id,card_id,reason}] for non-figure explanations. A grouped card can bind multiple DIFFERENT figures, each with its own exact matching aliases. Use aliases matching the actual accepted English words for that figure, including existing valid aliases, translated names and epithets. Do not bind 'Iapetus' to Atlas when a phrase means Iapetus's son. Preserve Doris mother/daughter scopes from the supplied cards; do not change verse scopes. For a single-figure card include its current matching label/aliases so no reachable explanation is lost. For grouped cards distribute names by figure; do not retain a whole multi-figure phrase on one figure. For collective cards that also name individuals (Horae/Eunomia/Dike/Eirene, Fates/Clotho/Lachesis/Atropos, Graces/Aglaia/Euphrosyne/Thalia), create entries and bindings for the collective AND each named figure. A personified plural such as Sorrows/Algea remains a collective, not invented named individuals.

Each entry requires at least one exact Greek excerpt from a cited verse label proving the identity in the local source. The first source appearance is useful; no need to quote every binding. Entire source and accepted cards are supplied. Output JSON only: entities, local_cards, uncertainties. Do not silently leave a card unclassified. Do not output the unchanged here text.
